Appendix A
Notation

If the summation or maximization symbol is not annotated with a range, it is understood by default to range from
1
to
N
. For example, we write

\sum_{i=1}^{N}
and
\max_{i=1}^{N}
as
\sum_{i}
and
\max_{i}
for short. We write
\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{N}\cdots\sum_{i_{l}=1}^{N}
as
\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}}
for short. We denote
\sum_{i_{1}\neq\cdots\neq i_{l}}
as the summation over all tuples of
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{l})
with mutually different
1\leq i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}\leq N
. We write
a_{n}\lesssim b_{n}
if there exists a constant
C
independent
N
such that
|a_{n}|\leq Cb_{n}
when
n
is large enough. We write
\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\star})=\sigma^{2}_{\star}
and
\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\star})=(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\star})^{2}
, for
\star\in\{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}\}
. Let
\textnormal{Cov}(\cdot)
denote the covariance. We use
\operatorname{E}
to denote the expectation and
E_{ij}
to denote the
(ij)
th element of the adjacency matrix of the network.

Appendix B
Unbiasedness and variance

B.1
Proof of
Proposition

1

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\Bigl\{\frac{Y_{i}Z_{i}}{r_{1}}-\frac{Y_{i}(1-Z_{i})}{r_{0}}\Bigr\},

Y_{i}=\alpha_{i}+\theta_{i}Z_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}.

Thus,

Y_{Z_{i}=z}=E(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i}=z)=\alpha_{i}+\theta_{i}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}r_{1}.

Therefore,

\operatorname{E}\Bigl\{\frac{Y_{i}Z_{i}}{r_{1}}-\frac{Y_{i}(1-Z_{i})}{r_{0}}\Bigr\}=Y_{Z_{i}=1}-Y_{Z_{i}=0}=\theta_{i},

which implies that
\operatorname{E}\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i}\theta_{i}=\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
.

To derive the formula of
\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})
, we see that

\displaystyle Y_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})=

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})+\theta_{i}Z_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})+\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}(Z_{i}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{i}r_{0}r_{1}+(\alpha_{i}+r_{0}\theta_{i}+\sum_{j}r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij})(Z_{i}-r_{1})+\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}(Z_{j}-r_{1})(Z_{i}-r_{1}).

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\frac{Y_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\frac{(Z_{i}-r_{1})}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}(\alpha_{i}+r_{0}\theta_{i}+\sum_{j}r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij})+\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}\frac{(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}.

(3)

Note that

\displaystyle r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}=\alpha_{i}+r_{0}\theta_{i}+\sum_{j}r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij},\quad\textnormal{Cov}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1},B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=0,

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(Z_{i}-r_{1})=r_{1}r_{0},\quad\textnormal{Var}\{(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})\}=(r_{1}r_{0})^{2},\quad i\neq j.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})=\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})+\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}.

∎

B.2
Proof of
Remark

3

We show the following proposition mentioned in
Remark

3
.

Proposition 7
.

When
\boldsymbol{E}=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{E}}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{\top}
, (i) if there exists a constant
C_{+}>0
, such that
\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}N_{i}\leq C_{+}N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
, then
\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
; (ii) if
\max_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}/\min_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}\leq C_{+}
, then
\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(1)
.

Before proving
Proposition

7
, we need
Lemma

1
below, coming from Theorem 0 of
Bell (1965)
.

Lemma 1

(Gershgorin Circle Theorem)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{A}=(A_{ij})
be a complex
n\times n
matrix. For
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, let
R_{i}
be the sum of the absolute values of the nondiagonal entries in the
i
th row:

R_{i}=\sum_{j:j\neq i}\left|A_{ij}\right|.

Let
D\left(A_{ii},R_{i}\right)\subseteq\mathbb{C}
be a closed disc centered at
A_{ii}
with radius
R_{i}
. Then, every eigenvalue of
\boldsymbol{A}
lies within at least one of the Gershgorin discs
D\left(A_{ii},R_{i}\right)
,
i=1,\ldots,N
.

Proof of
Proposition

7
.

For the first part of
Proposition

7
, when
\boldsymbol{E}=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}
equals to the maximum of absolute eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{E}
. Thus, by
Lemma

1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\max_{i}\sum_{j}E_{ij}=\max_{i}N_{i}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

For the second part of
Proposition

7
, when
\tilde{\boldsymbol{E}}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{E}}^{\top}
and
\max_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}/\min_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}\leq C_{+}
, we have
\|\textrm{diag}(\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1})\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O((N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})^{-1})
. Again by
Lemma

1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{E}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\textrm{diag}(\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1})\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\max_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}\cdot\big(\min_{i}\tilde{N}_{i}\big)^{-1}\leq C_{+}=O(1).

∎

B.3
Proof of
Proposition

2

Proof.

By Assumption
1
,
\max_{i}\max_{z\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|Y_{i}(z)|
has an upper bound
C_{Y}
. Consequently, we have

\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}(r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}C_{Y}^{2}=O(N^{-1}).

Recall that
{\boldsymbol{Q}}=(Q_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
is the normalized adjacency matrix of the latent network with
Q_{ij}=\tilde{E}_{ij}/\tilde{N}_{i}
if
\tilde{E}_{ij}=1
and
0
otherwise. Under Assumption
1
, we have
\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}\leq CQ_{ij}
. By Assumption
3
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{ii}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{-1}).

∎

B.4
Proof of
Proposition

3

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,j}E_{ij}\Bigl\{\frac{Y_{i}Z_{j}}{r_{1}}-\frac{Y_{i}(1-Z_{j})}{r_{0}}\Bigr\}.

Simple calculation gives

\displaystyle Y_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\theta_{i}Z_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik}Z_{k}(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})+r_{1}\theta_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\theta_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}r_{0}+\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik}Z_{k}(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r_{1}r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+(\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}r_{0}+r_{1}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+

\displaystyle\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik}(Z_{k}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\theta_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}Y_{i}(Z_{j}-r_{1})}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}(\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}r_{0}+r_{1}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik}(Z_{k}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\theta_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}(\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}r_{0}+r_{1}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sum_{j,k}\frac{E_{kj}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i:i\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\theta_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\frac{E_{ij}}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}(\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}r_{0}+r_{1}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})(Z_{j}-r_{1})+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{1}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i\neq j}\big(E_{ij}\theta_{i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\big)(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}.

(4)

Noting that
Y_{Z_{i}=z_{i}}^{Z_{j}=z_{j}}=\operatorname{E}(Y_{i}|Z_{i}=z_{i},Z_{j}=z_{j})
and

\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+r_{1}\sum_{k:k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik}=r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}^{Z_{j}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{i}=0}^{Z_{j}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{i}=1}^{Z_{j}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=0}^{Z_{j}=0},

(5)

for
i\neq j
, then we have
\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})=\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1})+\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2})=\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}+\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}.
By
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

3
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\Bigl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}(r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0})\Bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\Big)^{2}\lesssim N^{-2}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}\lesssim N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),

and

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}(E_{ij}\theta_{i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki})^{2}\lesssim\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}(E_{ij}\theta_{i})^{2}+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}E_{ij}+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}Q_{ki})^{2}=\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}Q_{ki})^{2}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\neq i}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}Q_{ki})^{2}\leq\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{k^{\prime}}E_{kj}Q_{ki}E_{k^{\prime}j}Q_{k^{\prime}i}\leq\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{k^{\prime}}Q_{ki}E_{k^{\prime}j}Q_{k^{\prime}i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{1}\leq N\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|^{2}_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Putting together the pieces, we have
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

∎

B.5
Proof of
Proposition

4

Proof.

It is easy to see that
E(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})=E(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})+E(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})=\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}+\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
. Moreover, by the proof of Propositions
1
–
3
, we can decompose
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
as
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}
, where
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}
and
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}
. Simple calculation gives
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{2}=\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1})
,
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{2}=\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2})
, and
\textnormal{Cov}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1},B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2})=0
. Therefore,
\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{2}
.

By Assumptions
1
–
3
and Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{2}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}+N^{-1})=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2});

\displaystyle\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{2}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1})=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

∎

B.6
Proof of
Proposition

5

Proof.

By replacing
Y_{Z_{j}=z}^{Z_{i}=z^{\prime}}
,
(z,z^{\prime})\in\{0,1\}^{2}
, with
e_{Z_{j}=z}^{Z_{i}=z^{\prime}}
in
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}
and
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}
, and replacing
\theta_{i}
with
\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}
in
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}
and
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})=(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}+(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2},\quad\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})=(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}+(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2},

where

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1})^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\Bigl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}(r_{1}r_{0}e_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}e_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}e_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}e_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0})\Bigr\}^{2},

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(E_{ij}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\Big)^{2}+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}+\sum_{k}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}_{kj}\Big)\Big(E_{ij}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\Big),

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\Bigl\{r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0}+

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{j}E_{ji}(r_{1}r_{0}e_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}e_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}e_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}e_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0})\Bigr\}^{2},

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}+\sum_{k}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}_{kj}\Big)\Big(\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}+E_{ij}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\Big)+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}+E_{ij}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\Big)^{2}.

Recall that
N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{I}_{K+1}
and

\displaystyle e_{i}=(\alpha_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i})+(\theta_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i})Z_{i}+\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}.

Define
\alpha_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}
as the projection of
\alpha_{i}
on
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\alpha}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i},\quad\text{where}\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\alpha_{i}.

Similarly, we define
\theta_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}
and
h_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}
as the linear projections of
\theta_{i}
and
h_{i}=\sum_{j}{\tilde{\gamma}}_{ij}
on
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
. Then,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}=\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=0}/N=\alpha_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}+r_{1}h_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}.

Therefore,

\alpha_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}=\alpha_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}-r_{1}h_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}.

Recall that

\theta_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}=\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}.

By (
5
) and replacing
\alpha_{i}
with
\alpha_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}-r_{1}h_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}
and
\theta_{i}
with
\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}
on the right hand side of

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}\Bigl\{\sum_{i}E_{ij}(\alpha_{i}+r_{1}\theta_{i}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+r_{1}\sum_{k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})\Bigr\}^{2},

we have

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}\Bigl\{\sum_{i}E_{ij}(\alpha_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}-r_{1}h_{\mid\boldsymbol{W},i}+r_{1}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+r_{1}\sum_{k\neq j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ik})\Bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}\Bigl\{\sum_{i}E_{ij}(\alpha_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+r_{1}h_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+r_{1}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+(r_{0}-r_{1})\tilde{\gamma}_{ij})\Bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}\Bigl\{\sum_{i}E_{ij}(r_{1}e_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}e_{Z_{i}=0}+(r_{0}-r_{1})\tilde{\gamma}_{ij})\Bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\Bigl\{\sum_{i}Q_{ij}\Bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}=O(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1}).

Moreover, by
Assumption

6
, similar to the way that we obtain the order of
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}
, we have

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(E_{ij}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}.

Putting together and noting that
N^{-1}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
by
Assumption

2
, we have
(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})^{2}=O(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1})=O(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}+(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=O(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1}),

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}+(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1})=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Therefore,
(\sigma^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})^{2}=(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}+(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{2}=O(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of the asymptotic normality

C.1
Notation and preliminary results

For a random variable
U
, let
\kappa_{4}(U)=\operatorname{E}(U^{4})-3\{\operatorname{E}(U^{2})\}^{2}
denote its fourth-order cumulant.
For one-variable function
f(i)
, let
\operatorname{Inf}_{i}(f)=f^{2}(i)
and
\left\|f\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=\{\sum_{i}f^{2}(i)\}^{1/2}
. For two-variable symmetric function
f(i,j)
that vanishes on diagonals, i.e.,
f(i,j)=f(j,i)
for
i\neq j
and
f(i,i)=0
, let
\operatorname{Inf}_{i}(f)=\sum_{j}f^{2}(i,j)
and
\left\|f\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=\{\sum_{i\neq j}f^{2}(i,j)\}^{1/2}
. Denote
\mathcal{M}(f)=\max_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}(f)
.

Lemma 2
.

Let
\{W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
be i.i.d random variables with
\operatorname{E}W_{i}=0
and
\operatorname{E}W_{i}^{2}=1
. Let
\{f_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
and
\{f_{ij}\}_{1\leq i\neq j\leq N}
be fixed arrays with
f_{ij}=f_{ji}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}\Big(\sum_{i}W_{i}f_{i}\Big)=\kappa_{4}(W_{1})\sum_{i}f_{i}^{4},\quad\kappa_{4}\Big(\sum_{i\neq j}W_{i}W_{j}f_{ij}\Big)=O(|M_{1}|+|M_{2}|+|M_{3}|+|M_{4}|),

where
M_{1}=\sum_{i\neq j}f_{ij}^{4}
,
M_{2}=\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}f_{ij}^{2}f_{ik}f_{jk}
,
M_{3}=\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}f_{ij}^{2}f_{ik}^{2}
, and
M_{4}=\sum_{i\neq j\neq k\neq l}f_{ik}f_{il}

f_{jk}f_{jl}
.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}\big(\sum_{i}W_{i}f_{i}\big)^{4}=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}f_{i}^{4}W_{i}^{4}+3\operatorname{E}\sum_{i\neq j}f_{i}^{2}f_{j}^{2}W_{i}^{2}W_{j}^{2}=\operatorname{E}W_{i}^{4}\sum_{i}f_{i}^{4}+3\sum_{i\neq j}f_{i}^{2}f_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle\Big\{\operatorname{E}\big(\sum_{i}W_{i}f_{i}\big)^{2}\Big\}^{2}=\big(\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}f_{i}^{2}W_{i}^{2}\big)^{2}=\big(\sum_{i}f_{i}^{2}\big)^{2}=\sum_{i}f_{i}^{4}+\sum_{i\neq j}f_{i}^{2}f_{j}^{2}.

As a consequence, we have

\kappa_{4}\big(\sum_{i}W_{i}f_{i}\big)=\kappa_{4}(W_{1})\sum_{i}f_{i}^{4}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}\big(\sum_{i\neq j}W_{i}W_{j}f_{ij}\big)^{4}=

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{i\neq j}f_{ij}^{4}+C_{2}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}f_{ij}^{2}f_{ik}^{2}+C_{3}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}f_{ij}^{2}f_{ik}f_{jk}+

\displaystyle 12\sum_{i\neq j\neq k\neq l}f_{ij}^{2}f_{kl}^{2}+C_{4}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k\neq l}f_{ik}f_{il}f_{jk}f_{jl},

\displaystyle\Big\{\operatorname{E}\big(\sum_{i\neq j}W_{i}W_{j}f_{ij}\big)^{2}\Big\}^{2}=

\displaystyle C_{5}\sum_{i\neq j}f_{ij}^{4}+C_{6}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}f_{ij}^{2}f_{ik}^{2}+4\sum_{i\neq j\neq k\neq l}f_{ij}^{2}f_{kl}^{2}.

Here,
C_{k}
,
1\leq k\leq 6
, are constants that do not depend on
N
. Their expressions are not crucial for our proof, so we omit them. As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}\big(\sum_{i\neq j}W_{i}W_{j}f_{ij}\big)=O(|M_{1}|+|M_{2}|+|M_{3}|+|M_{4}|).

∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
W_{i}=(Z_{i}-r_{1})/\sqrt{r_{1}r_{0}}
with
E(W_{i})=0
and
E(W_{i}^{2})=1
. Let
\{f_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
and
\{f_{ij}\}_{1\leq i\neq j\leq N}
be fixed arrays with
f_{ij}=f_{ji}
. Define
B_{1}=\sum_{i}f_{i}W_{i}
,
B_{2}=\sum_{i\neq j}f_{ij}W_{i}W_{j}
,
\sigma^{2}_{1}=\textnormal{Var}(B_{1})
, and
\sigma^{2}_{2}=\textnormal{Var}(B_{2})
. For
k=1,2
, if

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{k}^{2}>0
,
\kappa_{4}(B_{k})/\sigma_{k}^{4}\rightarrow 0
and
\mathcal{M}\left(f_{k}\right)/\sigma_{k}^{2}\rightarrow 0
, then

B_{k}/\sigma_{k}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Lemma
3
is a direct result of Theorem 1 of
De Jong (1990)
, so we omit its proof.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
1

Firstly, we prove
Proposition

8
below, which is useful for deriving the asymptotic normality of the DATE estimator.

Proposition 8
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
,

(i)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}N\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}>0
, then
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

(ii)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}N\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}>0
, then
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Proof.

Let
W_{i}=(Z_{i}-r_{1})/\sqrt{r_{1}r_{0}}
with
E(W_{i})=0
and
E(W_{i}^{2})=1
. Let
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)=(r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0})/(N\sqrt{r_{1}r_{0}})
and
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)=(\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ji})/(2N)
. Then
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}=\sum_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)W_{i}
and
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}=\sum_{i\neq j}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)W_{i}W_{j}
.

Step 1.
Proof of (i). By
Lemma

3
, we have

B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1),\quad k\in\{1,2\},

provided that the following two conditions hold: (1)
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k}^{4}\rightarrow 0
; and (2)

\mathcal{M}\left(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k}\right)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k}^{2}\rightarrow 0
.

By
Assumption

1
, we have
\max_{i}|f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)|=O(N^{-1})
. Moreover, by
Lemma

2
, we have

\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=O\big(\sum_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{4}(i)\big)=O(N^{-3}),\quad\mathcal{M}\left(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right)=\max_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}(i)=O(N^{-2}).

If
\liminf_{N}N\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}>0
, then
\mathcal{M}(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}=O(N^{-1})
and
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{4}=O(N^{-1})
. Therefore,
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Step 2.
Proof of (ii).
Denote
\bar{E}_{ij}=E_{ij}+E_{ji}\leq 2
,
\bar{Q}_{ij}=Q_{ij}+Q_{ji}\leq 2
, and
{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},ij}:=(\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}+\tilde{\gamma}_{ji})/(2N)\lesssim\bar{Q}_{ij}/N
. We write
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)
as
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},ij}
for short. Then
\|\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(1)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-2}\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\bar{Q}_{ij}^{2}\lesssim N^{-2}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}=O(N^{-1}),

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-2}\max_{i,j}\bar{Q}_{ij}^{2}=O(N^{-2}),

\displaystyle\max_{i}\sum_{j}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-2}\max_{i}\sum_{j}\bar{Q}_{ij}^{2}\leq N^{-2}\max_{i}(\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}^{\top})_{ii}=O(N^{-2}).

By
Lemma

2
, we have

\kappa_{4}\left({B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)=O\left(\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},21}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},22}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},23}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},24}\right|\right),

where

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},21}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{4}\leq\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}=O(N^{-3}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},22}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{2}i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{3}i_{1}}\lesssim N^{-4}\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bar{Q}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}\bar{Q}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\bar{Q}_{i_{2}i_{3}}\bar{Q}_{i_{3}i_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-4}\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bar{Q}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}\bar{Q}_{i_{2}i_{3}}\bar{Q}_{i_{3}i_{1}}\leq N^{-4}\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bar{Q}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{1}=O(N^{-3}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},23}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}\leq\max_{i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}=O(N^{-3}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},24}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}\neq i_{4}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{2}i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{3}i_{4}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{4}i_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}\neq i_{4}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{1}i_{2}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{2}i_{3}}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},i_{3}i_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-4}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{1}=O(N^{-3}).

Therefore, if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}N\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}>0
, then
\kappa_{4}({B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{4}=O(N^{-1})=o(1)
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)\lesssim N^{-2}\big(\max_{i}\sum_{j}Q_{ij}^{2}+\max_{i}\sum_{j}Q_{ji}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-2}\big(\|\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\|_{\operatorname{op}}+\|\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\big)=O(N^{-2}).

The conclusion follows.
∎

Next, we prove a general result for deriving the asymptotic normality of the unadjusted and EV-adjusted IATE and GATE estimators. Define

\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}+\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
with

\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,j}E_{ij}\Bigl\{\frac{Y_{i}^{\prime}Z_{j}}{r_{1}}-\frac{Y_{i}^{\prime}(1-Z_{j})}{r_{0}}\Bigr\},

where
Y_{i}^{\prime}=\alpha_{i}^{\prime}+\theta_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i}+\sum_{j}\tilde{E}_{ij}{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}Z_{j}
with
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ij}=\tilde{E}_{ij}{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}
. Here,
\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
are finite-population parameters like
\{\alpha_{i},\theta_{i},{\gamma}_{ij}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
. We define analogous quantites with respect to
\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
. For example, we define
\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
and
\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}
as the IATE and GATE for
\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
.

Note that
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}\equiv\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
when
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv Y_{i}
, and
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}\equiv\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
when
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv e_{i}
. Analogously, we define
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime~2}:=\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})
. Let
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime~2}
and
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime~2}
be the variances of linear and quadratic components of
\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
, respectively, similar to the definitions of
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{2}
and
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{2}
with
Y_{i}
replaced by
Y_{i}^{\prime}
. Then,
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime~2}=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime~2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime~2}
. Similarly, define
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~2}:=\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~2}
.

Proposition 9
.

Suppose that Assumption
1
holds for both
\{\alpha_{i},\theta_{i},{\gamma}_{ij}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
and

\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
, Assumption
2
–
4
holds, and for a nonrandom sequence
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=1}\Big)^{2},\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=0}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=O(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=1}\Big)^{2},\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=0}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=o(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Then, we have

(i)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, we have
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

(ii)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, we have
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

(iii)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime~2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, we have
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

(iv)

if
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime~2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, we have
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Proof.

Step 1.
We prove (i). With a slight abuse of notation, let

f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}(i)=\sum_{j}E_{ji}\{r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime}+(r_{0}-r_{1})\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}^{\prime}\}/(N\sqrt{r_{1}r_{0}}).

Note that
\liminf_{N}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
,
\sum_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{2}(i)=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}
, and

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime}\Big)^{2},\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=O(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime}\Big)^{2},\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=o(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{2}(i)}{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}}\lesssim\frac{N^{-2}\max_{i}\{(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime})^{2},(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime})^{2},\sum_{j}Q_{ji}^{2}\}}{N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}}=o(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{4}(i)\leq\max_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{2}(i)\sum_{i}f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{2}(i)=o(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~4}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~4}\rightarrow 0,\quad\mathcal{M}\left(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}\right)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}\rightarrow 0.

As a consequence, by
Lemma

3
, we have

B^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Step 2.
We prove (ii). With a slight abuse of notation, let
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,i)=0
and

f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,j)=(E_{ij}\theta^{\prime}_{i}+E_{ji}\theta^{\prime}_{j}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ki}+\sum_{k}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{kj})/(2N),\quad i\neq j.

Write
f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,j)
as
{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}
for short. Let
g_{i}=\sum_{j}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ki}+\sum_{k}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{kj})^{2}
. Then,

\displaystyle g_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j}(\sum_{k}E_{kj}|\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ki}|)^{2}+\sum_{j}(\sum_{k}E_{ki}|\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{kj}|)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{ki}E_{li}Q_{kj}Q_{lj}+\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{kj}E_{lj}Q_{ki}Q_{li}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E})_{ii}+(\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q})_{ii}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\max_{i}g_{i}\lesssim 2\|\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})=o(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

(6)

where the last equality follows from
Assumption

2
(
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=o(1)
). Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}g_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{ki}E_{li}Q_{kj}Q_{lj}+\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{kj}E_{lj}Q_{ki}Q_{li}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{li}Q_{kj}Q_{lj}+\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}E_{lj}Q_{ki}Q_{li}

\displaystyle\lesssim N\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}g_{i}^{2}\leq(\max_{i}g_{i})\Big(\sum_{i}g_{i}\Big)=O(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{3})=o(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}).

(7)

If follows from
Assumption

4
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}\right)\lesssim\max_{i}N^{-2}\big(\sum_{j}E_{ij}\theta_{i}^{\prime~2}+\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{j}^{\prime~2}+g_{i}\big)

\displaystyle\leq N^{-2}\big\{O(\max_{i}N_{i})+O(\max_{i}M_{i})+O(\max_{i}g_{i})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-2}\big\{o(N^{3/2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})+o(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})\big\}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\max_{i}\sum_{j}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}\left({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}\right)=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),\quad\max_{i,j}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}^{2}\leq\max_{i}\sum_{j}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}^{2}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\sum_{k}|{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},jk}||{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ki}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j}\sum_{k}({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},jk}^{2}+{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ki}^{2})/2

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\sum_{k}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ki}^{2}+\max_{j}\sum_{k}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},jk}^{2}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~2}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

By
Lemma

2
, we have
\kappa_{4}\left(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}\right)=O\left(\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},21}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},22}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},23}\right|+\left|M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},24}\right|\right)
, where

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},21}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{4}\leq\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},22}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{3}i_{1}}\leq\max_{i_{1},i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}|{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}||{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{3}i_{1}}|\cdot\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},23}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}\leq\max_{i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}^{2}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},24}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq i_{3}\neq i_{4}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{3}i_{4}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{4}i_{1}}.

Next, we derive the magnitude of
M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},24}
. Let
\boldsymbol{G}=\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}
,
\bar{G}_{ij}={G}_{ij}+{G}_{ji}
,
\bar{E}_{ij}={E}_{ij}+{E}_{ji}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{G}}=(\bar{G}_{ij})_{i,j}
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{E}}=(\bar{E}_{ij})_{i,j}
, then we have
|{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},ij}|\leq CN^{-1}(\bar{G}_{ij}+\bar{E}_{ij})
, for some constant
C
, and

\displaystyle M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},24}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{1}i_{2}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{2}i_{3}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{3}i_{4}}{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i_{4}i_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-4}\operatorname{tr}\big\{(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})\big\}.

The above trace can be decomposed into

\sum_{\boldsymbol{D}_{1},\boldsymbol{D}_{2},\boldsymbol{D}_{3},\boldsymbol{D}_{4}\in\{\boldsymbol{\bar{E}},\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\}^{4}}\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{D}_{1}\boldsymbol{D}_{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{3}\boldsymbol{D}_{4}).

(i)

At least one
\boldsymbol{D}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}
for
i=1,2,3,4
. Without loss of generality, supposing
\boldsymbol{D}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}
, then we have

\operatorname{tr}(\bar{\boldsymbol{E}}\boldsymbol{D}_{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{3}\boldsymbol{D}_{4})\lesssim\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}_{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{3}\boldsymbol{D}_{4}\boldsymbol{1}\leq N\|\boldsymbol{D}_{2}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{3}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{4}\|_{\operatorname{op}}.

Note that
\|\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{{E}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
,
\|\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{{Q}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{{E}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
, then

N\|\boldsymbol{D}_{2}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{3}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{D}_{4}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=NO(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})\cdot O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})\cdot O(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=O(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{3}).

(ii)

No term in the summation equals
\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle\leq N\|\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\bar{G}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\bar{Q}}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{3}).

In summary, we obtain that

\operatorname{tr}\big((\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})(\boldsymbol{\bar{E}}+\boldsymbol{\bar{G}})\big)=O(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{3}).

Therefore,
M_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},24}=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{3})=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})
, and thus,
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})
. If
\liminf_{N}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}

+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, we have
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~4}=o(1)
. As a consequence, by
Lemma

3
, we have

B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Step 3.
The proofs of (iii) and (iv) are similar to those of (i) and (ii), so we omit them.
∎

Based on these preliminary results, we can prove Theorem
1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Step 1.
We prove the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
. Recall the decomposition (
3
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}.

Moreover,

\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}=O(N^{-1})
and
\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}=O(N^{-1}).

Consider the decomposition
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{-1}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
, where
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2}=\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})
. Let
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
,
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
,
\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)=f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
, and
\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)=f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
, then we have
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}=\sum_{i}\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}(i)W_{i}
,
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}=\sum_{i\neq j}\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}(i,j)

W_{i}W_{j}
.

Let
\mathbf{G}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1},G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})
be a normal approximation of
(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1},\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})
, i.e.,
G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}
and
G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}
are two independent zero-mean normal random variables with variances
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2}
and
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2}
, respectively.

By Theorem 2.1 of
Koike (2022)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\textnormal{P}\left(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\leq x_{1};\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x_{2}\right)-\textnormal{P}\left(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\leq x_{1};G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},0}\left(\delta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\delta_{1}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\max_{k=1,2}\left(\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k})\right)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(1+\frac{1}{\min\left\{\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})^{1/2},\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})^{1/2}\right\}}\right),

where
C_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},0}
is a constant that does not depend on
N
and

\displaystyle\delta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})=

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{cov}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})-\operatorname{cov}(\boldsymbol{G}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\delta_{1}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})=

\displaystyle\left(\left|\kappa_{4}\left(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right)\right|+\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left(\left|\kappa_{4}\left(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)\right|+\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}\left(\left|\kappa_{4}\left(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)\right|+\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/4}.

Note that
\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k})=\textnormal{Var}(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k})
for
k=1,2
, and
\operatorname{Cov}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1},\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=0=\operatorname{Cov}(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1},G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})
. Therefore,

\delta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})=0.

We have shown in
Proposition

8
that
\mathcal{M}({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=O(N^{-2})
,
\mathcal{M}({f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=O(N^{-2})
,
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=O(N^{-3})
, and
\kappa_{4}({B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=O(N^{-3})
. Since
\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}={f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=O(N^{-1})
,
\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=O(N^{-1})
,
\kappa_{4}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})=O(N^{-1})
, and
\kappa_{4}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=O(N^{-1})
.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{4}(i)=O\big(\kappa_{4}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1})\big)=O(N^{-1}),

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{i}\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}(i)\Big)^{1/2}=\left(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}=O(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}(i,j)\Big)^{2}\leq\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}+\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}^{2}}{2N^{2}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-4}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{-4}\Big\{\sum_{i}\big(\sum_{j}Q_{ij}^{2}\big)^{2}+\sum_{i}\big(\sum_{j}Q_{ji}^{2}\big)^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-4}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{-4}\Big(\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}Q_{ij}Q_{ik}+\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{k}Q_{ji}Q_{ki}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq N^{-4}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{-4}\big(\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{1}+\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}\big)=O(N^{-1}).

In summary, we have

\left(\delta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\delta_{1}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\max_{k=1,2}\left(\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},k})\right)^{1/2}\right)=O(N^{-1/12}).

By Assumption
5
, we have either
\liminf_{N}N\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}>0
or
\liminf_{N}N\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}^{2}>0
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
\liminf_{N}N\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}^{2}>0
. Then we split
\{\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})\}_{N=1,2,...}
into two subsequences. The first subsequence satisfies
\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})<N^{-1/7}
, such that
\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})=o(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}^{2})=o(N^{-1})
. The CLT, i.e.,
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, holds in this case by Slutsky Theorem and
Proposition

8
. The second subsequence satisfies
\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2})\geq N^{-1/7}
, and then

\displaystyle\sup_{\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\textnormal{P}\big(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\leq x_{1};\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x_{2}\big)-\textnormal{P}\big(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}\leq x_{1};G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x_{2}\big)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(N^{-1/12})\cdot O\bigg(1+\frac{1}{N^{-1/14}}\bigg)=o(1),

which implies

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\textnormal{P}\big(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x\big)-\textnormal{P}\big(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}\leq x\big)\right|=o(1).

Therefore, we always have

\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},1}+\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}},2}=\frac{\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}}{\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})^{1/2}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Step 2.
We prove the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
and
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
. We will prove a more general result stated in
Theorem

7
below.

Theorem 7
.

Suppose that Assumption
1
holds for both
\{\alpha_{i},\theta_{i},{\gamma}_{ij}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
and

\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
, Assumptions
2
–
4
hold, and for a nonrandom sequence
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=1}\Big)^{2},\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=0}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=O(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=1}\Big)^{2},\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=0}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=o(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Then, we have

(i)

if

\text{ either }\liminf{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}}/{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})}>0\text{ or }\liminf{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~2}}/{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})}>0,

then
(\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}-\tau^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})/\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})^{1/2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

(ii)

if
\text{ either }\liminf{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},1}^{\prime~2}}/{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})}>0\text{ or }\liminf{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}},2}^{\prime~2}}/{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})}>0,
then
(\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime})/\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime})^{1/2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Recall that
M_{i}=\sum_{j}E_{ji}
. By Assumptions
1
–
4
, we have,

N^{-1}\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=z}\Big)^{2}\lesssim N^{-1}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),\quad z=0,1,

N^{-1}\Big\{\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=z}\Big)^{2}\Big\}\lesssim N^{-1}\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}M_{i}^{2}=o(N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}),\quad z=0,1.

Thus, the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
and
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
follows by taking
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}
and
Y^{\prime}_{i}\equiv Y_{i}
in
Theorem

7
. In the remainder of this section, we will prove
Theorem

7
. We will only prove
Theorem

7
(i), as the proof of
Theorem

7
(ii) is almost the same.

We have shown in (
5
) that (with
i
and
j
exchanged)

\alpha_{j}+r_{1}\theta_{j}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}+r_{1}\sum_{k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}=r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}.

Moreover, we have

r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}=\alpha_{j}+r_{1}\theta_{j}+\sum_{k}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}r_{1}.

Similar to the decomposition of
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
in (
4
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\frac{\left(Z_{i}-r_{1}\right)}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}E_{ji}\big(r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime Z_{i}=0}\big),

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\frac{\left(Z_{i}-r_{1}\right)}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\sum_{j}E_{ji}\big\{r_{1}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=1}+r_{0}Y^{\prime}_{Z_{j}=0}+(r_{0}-r_{1})\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ji}\big\},

\displaystyle B^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\left(Z_{i}-r_{1}\right)\left(Z_{j}-r_{1}\right)}{Nr_{1}r_{0}}\big(E_{ij}\theta^{\prime}_{i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{ki}\big).

Mimicking the proof of
Proposition

5
with
e_{i}\equiv Y_{i}^{\prime}
and
\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}=\textnormal{Var}(B^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1})=O(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),\quad\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}=\textnormal{Var}(B^{\prime}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2})=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Let
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
,
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
,
\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}(i)=f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}(i)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
, and
\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,j)=f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,j)/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
. By the assumption of
\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
and the proof of
Proposition

9
, we have
\mathcal{M}(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1})=o(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~4})
,
\mathcal{M}(f_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2})=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
,
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime})=o(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~4})
, and
\kappa_{4}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})
. Since
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i}^{\prime}=B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i})=o(1)
and
\kappa_{4}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},i}^{\prime})=o(1)
,
i=1,2
. It is easy to verify that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}\right)^{2}=o(1),~\left\|\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}\right\|_{\ell_{2}}=O(1).

By Assumption
3
, we have

\sum_{i}N_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i}(\sum_{j}E_{ij})^{2}\leq N\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{3}\rho_{N}^{2}),

\sum_{i}M_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i}(\sum_{j}E_{ji})^{2}\leq N\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}=O(N^{3}\rho_{N}^{2}).

Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\operatorname{Inf}_{i}\left(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\Big\{\sum_{j}\big(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}(i,j)\big)^{2}\Big\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-4}(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime})^{-4}\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ij}\theta_{i}^{\prime 2}+\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{j}^{\prime 2}+g_{i}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-4}(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime})^{-4}\sum_{i}\Big\{\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ij}\theta_{i}^{\prime 2}\Big)^{2}+\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{j}^{\prime 2}\Big)^{2}+g_{i}^{2}\Big\},

\displaystyle\leq(N^{2}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}+N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})^{-1}\Big\{O\Big(\sum_{i}N_{i}^{2}\Big)+O\Big(\sum_{i}M_{i}^{2}\Big)+O\Big(\sum_{i}g_{i}^{2}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq(N^{2}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}+N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})^{-1}\big\{O(N^{3}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})+O(N^{3}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})+o(N^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})\big\}=o(1),

where the last inequality is due to (
7
).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}:=

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\delta_{1}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{B}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{3}}+\max_{k=1,2}\left(\mathcal{M}(\tilde{f}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},k})\right)^{1/2}\right)=o(1).

We know that either
\liminf_{N}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
or
\liminf_{N}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
\liminf_{N}\sigma^{\prime~2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}/(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})>0
, and then we split
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}
into two subsequences.

The first subsequence satisfies
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}\leq(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{1/3}
. Then,
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}=o(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=o(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~2})
. Therefore,
B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{P}0
and
\textnormal{Var}(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~2}\to 1
. By
Proposition

9
and Slutsky’s throrem, we have
(B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}+B_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

The second subsequence satisfies
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}>(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{1/3}
, then we have

\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{\min\left\{\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime})^{1/2},\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})^{1/2}\right\}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 1+\frac{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})^{1/2}}{\min\left\{(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})^{1/2}\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{1/6},(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})^{1/2}\right\}}=O(\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{-1/6}).

By Theorem 2.1 of
Koike (2022)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\textnormal{P}\big(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}\leq x_{1};\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}\leq x_{2}\big)-\textnormal{P}\big(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}\leq x_{1};G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}\leq x_{2}\big)\right|=O(\delta_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{5/6}),

where
(G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1},G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2})
are normal approximation of
(\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime},\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime})
, i.e.,
G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}
and
G_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}
are two independent zero-mean normal random variables with variances
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime~2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~2}
and
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime~2}/\sigma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime~2}
, respectively.

Therefore, we always have
\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},1}^{\prime}+\tilde{B}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}},2}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

∎

Appendix D
Consistency of variance estimator

D.1
Some useful lemmas

Lemma

4
below is from
Steele (1986)
.

Lemma 4

(Efron-Stein inequality)
.

Let
S_{i}
,
S_{i}^{\prime}
,
i=1,\ldots,N
, be
2N
i.i.d. random variables. Let
\boldsymbol{S}=(S_{1},\ldots,S_{N})
and
\boldsymbol{S}^{(i)}=(S_{1},\ldots,S_{i-1},S_{i}^{\prime},S_{i+1},\ldots,S_{N})
. For any measurable function
f
, we have

\textnormal{Var}\{f(\boldsymbol{S})\}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}\operatorname{E}\{f(\boldsymbol{S})-f(\boldsymbol{S}^{(i)})\}^{2}.

Lemma
5
below is a direct result of
Lemma

4
.

Lemma 5
.

Let
Z_{i}
,
Z_{i}^{\prime}
,
i=1,\ldots,N
, be
2N
i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables. Let
c_{j}=\max_{\boldsymbol{z}_{(-j)}\in\{0,1\}^{N-1}}|f(\boldsymbol{z})-f(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})|
where
\boldsymbol{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}=1,\ldots,z_{n})
,
\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}=0,\ldots,z_{N})
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{(-j)}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j-1},z_{j+1}\ldots,z_{N})
. Then, we have

\textnormal{Var}\{f(\boldsymbol{Z})\}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}c_{j}^{2}.

D.2
Proof of Theorem
2

Proof.

Step 1.
We prove (i). Note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}Y_{i}^{2}Z_{i}=r_{1}\operatorname{E}(Y_{i}^{2}\mid Z_{i}=1)=r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=1}^{2}+r_{1}\textnormal{Var}(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i}=1),

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}Y_{i}^{2}(1-Z_{i})=r_{0}\operatorname{E}(Y_{i}^{2}\mid Z_{i}=0)=r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=0}^{2}+r_{0}\textnormal{Var}(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i}=0).

Using the independence of
Z_{i}
’s and that
Y_{i}=\alpha_{i}+\theta_{i}Z_{i}+\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}Z_{j}
, we have

\textnormal{Var}(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i}=0)=\textnormal{Var}(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i}=1)=\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}\textnormal{Var}(Z_{j})=\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}r_{0}r_{1}.

Putting together the pieces, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\operatorname{E}Y_{i}^{2}Z_{i}/r_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\operatorname{E}Y_{i}^{2}(1-Z_{i})/r_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=1}^{2}/r_{1}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=0}^{2}/r_{0}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}

(8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\big\{(r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0})^{2}/r_{0}r_{1}+(Y_{Z_{i}=0}-Y_{Z_{i}=1})^{2}\big\}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\big\{(r_{0}Y_{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{i}=0})^{2}/r_{0}r_{1}+\theta_{i}^{2}\big\}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}.

Comparing with the expression of
\sigma^{2}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}
, we have

\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}-\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\theta_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\sum_{i}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}(2\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})\geq 0

follows from

\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}\geq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\sum_{i}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}.

Step 2.
We prove (ii). We first show that

N^{-1}\sum_{i}Z_{i}Y_{i}^{2}=\operatorname{E}\Big(N^{-1}\sum_{i}Z_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\Big)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Let
\boldsymbol{Z}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})
. Define
f(\boldsymbol{Z})=f(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}):=\sum_{i}Z_{i}Y_{i}^{2}
. Let
\boldsymbol{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}=1,\ldots,z_{n})
,
\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}=0,\ldots,z_{n})
, and
\boldsymbol{z}_{(-j)}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{j-1},z_{j+1}\ldots,z_{n})
. By Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{z}_{(-j)}\in\{0,1\}^{N-1}}|f(\boldsymbol{z})-f(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})|\lesssim|Y_{j}^{2}(\boldsymbol{z})|+\sum_{i:i\neq j}|Y_{i}^{2}(\boldsymbol{z})-Y_{i}^{2}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})|\lesssim 1+\sum_{i}Q_{ij}.

As a consequence, by
Lemma

5
, we have

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(f(\boldsymbol{Z}))\lesssim\sum_{j}\Big(1+\sum_{i}Q_{ij}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\sum_{j}1+\sum_{j}\Big(\sum_{i}Q_{ij}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=N+\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}\leq N+N\|\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\lesssim N,

which yields

\textnormal{Var}\Big(N^{-1}\sum_{i}Z_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\Big)=O(N^{-1})=o(1).

Similarly,

N^{-1}\sum_{i}(1-Z_{i})Y_{i}^{2}=\operatorname{E}\Big\{N^{-1}\sum_{i}(1-Z_{i})Y_{i}^{2}\Big\}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The conclusion follows.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof.

Step 1.
We prove (i). We decompose
\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3},

where

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j^{\prime}}}{N^{2}r_{1}^{3}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{Z_{i}(1-Z_{j})(1-Z_{j^{\prime}})}{N^{2}r_{1}^{2}r_{0}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{(1-Z_{i})Z_{j}Z_{j^{\prime}}}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}^{2}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{(1-Z_{i})(1-Z_{j})(1-Z_{j^{\prime}})}{N^{2}r_{0}^{3}}

\displaystyle=:S_{11}+S_{12}+S_{13}+S_{14},

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}Z_{j}}{N^{2}r_{1}^{2}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}(1-Z_{j})}{N^{2}r_{1}^{2}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{(1-Z_{i})Z_{j}}{N^{2}r_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{(1-Z_{i})(1-Z_{j})}{N^{2}r_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=:S_{21}+S_{22}+S_{23}+S_{24},

\displaystyle S_{3}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{r_{0}Z_{i}Z_{j}}{N^{2}r_{1}^{3}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}(1-Z_{j})}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{(1-Z_{i})Z_{j}}{N^{2}r_{1}r_{0}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{E}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{r_{1}(1-Z_{i})(1-Z_{j})}{N^{2}r_{0}^{3}}

\displaystyle=:S_{31}+S_{32}+S_{33}+S_{34}.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{1}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}\biggr)^{2},\quad\tilde{S}_{2}=\frac{r_{0}}{N^{2}r_{1}}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{3}=\frac{r_{1}}{N^{2}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}\biggr)^{2},\quad\tilde{S}_{4}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}\biggr)^{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{5}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}(E_{ij}\theta_{i}+\sum_{k}E_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{ki})^{2}.

For
z,z^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
and
i\neq j\neq j^{\prime}
, simple calculation yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{{I}(Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})}{\operatorname{E}{I}(Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})}=\operatorname{E}(Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}(Y_{j}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})\operatorname{E}(Y_{j^{\prime}}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\textnormal{Cov}(Y_{j},Y_{j^{\prime}}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}Y_{j}^{2}\frac{{I}(Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime})}{\operatorname{E}{I}(Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime})}=\operatorname{E}(Y_{j}^{2}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Y_{Z_{j}=z^{\prime}}^{Z_{i}=z})^{2}+\textnormal{Var}(Y_{j}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\textnormal{Cov}(Y_{j},Y_{j^{\prime}}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})=\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0},

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(Y_{j}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime})=\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}(Y_{j}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=Z_{j^{\prime}}=z^{\prime})=\operatorname{E}(Y_{j}\mid Z_{i}=z,Z_{j}=z^{\prime})+(z^{\prime}-r_{1})\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}.

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle S_{11}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\operatorname{E}\Big(Y_{j}Y_{j^{\prime}}\frac{Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{j^{\prime}}}{N^{2}r_{1}^{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}})(Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0},

and

\displaystyle S_{21}=\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\operatorname{E}\Big(Y_{j}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}Z_{j}}{N^{2}r_{1}^{2}}\Big)=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}(Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1})^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}{E}_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

Comparing with
\tilde{S}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle S_{11}+S_{21}=

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{1}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

(9)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle S_{12}+S_{22}=

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{2}+\frac{r_{0}}{N^{2}r_{1}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(-r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}-r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}}{N^{2}r_{1}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0}+\frac{r_{0}}{N^{2}r_{1}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

(10)

\displaystyle S_{13}+S_{23}=

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{3}+\frac{r_{1}}{N^{2}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=1}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}}{N^{2}r_{0}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0}+\frac{r_{1}}{N^{2}r_{0}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

(11)

\displaystyle S_{14}+S_{24}=

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{4}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(-r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}-r_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{j^{\prime}}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}r_{1}r_{0}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}r_{1}r_{0}.

Summating (

)–(

) and using

for

\displaystyle S_{1}+S_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{4}\tilde{S}_{q}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}(\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}\theta_{j}+\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}+\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}k}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}.

(13)

On the other hand, we can verify that

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{5}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\theta_{j}^{2}E_{ji}+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\sum_{k:k\neq i}E_{ji}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}_{kj}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{k}E_{ki}\tilde{\gamma}_{kj}^{2}+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\sum_{k^{\prime}\neq k}E_{ki}{E}_{k^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{kj}\tilde{\gamma}_{k^{\prime}j}.

Substituting (

\displaystyle S_{1}+S_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{5}\tilde{S}_{q}-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\theta_{j}^{2}E_{ji}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}E_{ji}E_{j^{\prime}i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jj^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{j^{\prime}j}.

Noting that, for

\displaystyle=(r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=z}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=z})^{2}+r_{1}r_{0}(Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=z})^{2}

we have

\displaystyle S_{31}+S_{33}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{1}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\left\{r_{1}(Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=z})^{2}+r_{0}(Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1})^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{r_{0}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}r_{1}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\left\{r_{1}r_{0}\theta_{j}^{2}+(r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1})^{2}\right\}+r_{0}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2},

\displaystyle S_{32}+S_{34}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{0}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\left\{r_{1}(Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}+r_{0}(Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{0})^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{r_{1}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{0}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\left\{r_{1}r_{0}\theta_{j}^{2}+(r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{r_{1}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}.

Simple calculation yields

\displaystyle r_{0}(r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1})^{2}+r_{1}(r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}+r_{1}r_{0}(r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}-

\displaystyle r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}-r_{0}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}+r_{1}r_{0}\gamma_{ji}^{2}.

Combing these, we have

\displaystyle S_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\left\{\theta_{j}^{2}+\gamma_{ji}^{2}+(r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}/(r_{1}r_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\sum_{k:k\neq i}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{1}r_{0}N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}(r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=1}+r_{0}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=1}r_{1}^{2}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{Z_{i}=0}+r_{0}r_{1}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{Z_{i}=0})^{2}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}E_{ji}\big(\theta_{j}^{2}+\sum_{k}\tilde{\gamma}_{jk}^{2}\big).

(16)

Combining (
15
) and (
