\displaystyle N^{-1}\max\Big\{\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=1}^{\prime}\Big)^{2},\max_{i=1,\ldots,N}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{Z_{j}=0}^{\prime}\Big)^{2}\Big\}=o(\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

we have
\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}-\operatorname{E}(\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
.

Proof of
Theorem

10
.

Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}-\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\prime}-\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{DIR}}}=S_{4}+S_{5}+S_{6}+S_{7}.

where

\displaystyle S_{4}=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{i}Z_{i}\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}^{2}},\quad S_{5}=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{i}(1-Z_{i})\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}^{2}},

\displaystyle S_{6}=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{i}Z_{i}\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}\frac{1-Z_{j}}{r_{0}^{2}},\quad S_{7}=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}Y_{i}(1-Z_{i})\sum_{j}E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}\frac{1-Z_{j}}{r_{0}^{2}}.

Define
f(\boldsymbol{Z})=f(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}):=S_{4}
. Let
\boldsymbol{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k}=1,\ldots,z_{n})
,
\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k}=0,\ldots,z_{n})
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{(-k)}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k-1},z_{k+1}\ldots,z_{n})
, then, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{z}_{(-k)}\in\{0,1\}^{N-1}}|f(\boldsymbol{z})-f(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j:i\neq k,j\neq k}\Big|Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})-Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z})E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z})\Big|

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{j}\Big|Y_{k}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})E_{jk}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})-Y_{k}(\boldsymbol{z})E_{jk}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z})\Big|

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Big|Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})E_{ki}Y_{k}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})-Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z})E_{ki}Y_{k}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z})\Big|.

Since both
\{\alpha_{i},\theta_{i},{\gamma}_{ij}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
and
\{\alpha_{i}^{\prime},\theta_{i}^{\prime},{\gamma}_{ij}^{\prime}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
satisfy
Assumption

1
, we have

\max_{\boldsymbol{z}\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z})|=O(1)
,
\max_{\boldsymbol{z}\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|Y_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z})|=O(1)
, and for
k\not\in\{i,j\}
,

|Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})-Y_{i}(\boldsymbol{z})E_{ji}Y_{j}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{z})|\lesssim\bar{E}_{ji}(Q_{ik}+Q_{jk}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{z}_{(-k)}\in\{0,1\}^{N-1}}|f(\boldsymbol{z})-f(\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})|\lesssim\sum_{i,j}\bar{E}_{ji}(Q_{ik}+Q_{jk})+\sum_{i}E_{ki}+\sum_{j}E_{jk},

and

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(S_{4})\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{-4}\sum_{k}\Big(\sum_{i}E_{ki}\Big)^{2}+N^{-4}\sum_{k}\Big(\sum_{j}E_{jk}\Big)^{2}+N^{-4}\sum_{k}\Big(\sum_{i}\sum_{j}E_{ji}Q_{ik}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+N^{-4}\sum_{k}\Big(\sum_{i}\sum_{j}E_{ji}Q_{jk}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{-4}\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{1}+N^{-4}\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}+N^{-4}\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}+N^{-4}\boldsymbol{1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{-1}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}=o(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}).

Therefore,
S_{4}=\operatorname{E}S_{4}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
. Similarly, we can prove that, for all
6\leq j\leq 7
,
S_{j}=\operatorname{E}S_{j}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
. Together with Theorem
2
(ii) and Theorem
8
, we have

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{E}\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\prime}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

∎

Proof of
Theorem

4
.

Theorem
4
(i) follows from
Theorem

9
with
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv Y_{i}
. Theorem
4
(ii) follows from
Theorem

10
with
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv N^{2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2}
and
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv Y_{i}
.

∎

Appendix E
Proof for the asymptotic results of the eigenvector-adjusted estimator

E.1
Preliminary results

Lemma 6
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
3
, and
9
, we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{z,\textrm{ora}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2})
, for
z=0,1
.

Proof.

By definition, the adjustment coefficient is

\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{z}=\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\sum_{i}(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i})^{2}I(Z_{i}=z)=\Big(\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{i}.

Simple calculation yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}=\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i}Z_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})\boldsymbol{W}_{i}(\alpha_{i}+\theta_{i}+r_{1}h_{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1})\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\Big(\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}\Big)+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i,j}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1})\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}.

By
Assumption

9
and
N^{-1}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{I}_{K+1}
, we have

(Nr_{1})^{-1}\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{I}_{K+1}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}).

Let
{W}_{ik}
be the
k
th element of
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
. By
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

9
, we can verify that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1}){W}_{ik}(\alpha_{i}+\theta_{i}+r_{1}h_{i})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i,j}(Z_{i}-r_{1})(Z_{j}-r_{1}){W}_{ik}\tilde{\gamma}_{ij}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}\Big\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}(Z_{i}-r_{1}){W}_{jk}\Big(\sum_{j}\tilde{\gamma}_{ji}\Big)\Big\}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}Q_{ji}\Big)^{2}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{1}=O(N^{-1}).

As a consequence, we have

\frac{1}{Nr_{1}}\sum_{i:Z_{i}=1}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}).

Putting together the pieces, we have
(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2})
. Similarly, we have
(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2})
.
∎

Theorem 11
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
and
6
–
8
, we have
(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})/\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{1/2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})/\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{1/2}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Proof.

The asymptotic normality of
\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
and
\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
follows from
Theorem

7
with
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
and
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv e_{i}
.
∎

E.2
Proof of
Theorem

5

Proof.

Simple calculation yields

\displaystyle e_{i}-\hat{e}_{i}=Z_{i}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}+(1-Z_{i})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,j}E_{ij}\Bigl\{\frac{(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}Z_{j}}{r_{1}}-\frac{(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}(1-Z_{j})}{r_{0}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2})O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{1/2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{1/2}),

where the second equality is due to
Lemma

6
and the last equality is due to
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\rightarrow 0
by
Assumption

2
.

By
Assumption

7
(i),
Theorem

11
, and Slutsky’s Theorem, we have

(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})/\textnormal{Var}(\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}})^{1/2}

\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. The proof for the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}
is similar, so we omit it.

∎

E.3
Proof of
Theorem

6

Proof.

Step 1.
We prove (i). Simple calculation yields

\displaystyle N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\hat{e}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr)^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}({e}_{j}+\hat{e}_{j}-e_{j})\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}{e}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr)^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{r_{1}}+N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}(\hat{e}_{j}-e_{j})\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{r_{1}}+

\displaystyle N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}(\hat{e}_{j}-e_{j})\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}{e}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr)\frac{Z_{i}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}.

By
Assumption

9
, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{N^{2}r_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})^{\top}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}\biggl\{\sum_{j}E_{ji}\boldsymbol{W}_{j}^{\top}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr\}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1,\textrm{ora}})\frac{Z_{i}}{N^{2}r_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1})O_{\mathbb{P}}(N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{2})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Therefore,
S_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
. By the proof of
Theorem

8
with
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv e_{i}
and
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
, we have

S_{1}=\operatorname{E}S_{1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

By Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have
S_{3}\lesssim(S_{1}S_{2})^{1/2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)
. Therefore,

N^{-2}\sum_{i}\biggl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\hat{e}_{j}\frac{Z_{j}}{r_{1}}\biggr)^{2}\frac{Z_{i}}{N^{2}r_{1}}=\operatorname{E}S_{1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

Define
\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}
by replacing
Y_{i}
in
\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
with the oracle residual
e_{i}
. Mimicking the above proof for the other terms of
\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}
, we can obtain that

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)=\operatorname{E}\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big),

(25)

where the last equality is due to
Theorem

8
with
\Lambda_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\equiv\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
and
Y_{i}^{\prime}\equiv e_{i}
.
Similarly,

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)=\operatorname{E}\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big).

By the proof of
Theorem

3
with
Y_{i}
replaced by
e_{i}
, we have
\operatorname{E}(2\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})\geq 0
and

\displaystyle\operatorname{E}\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}\Bigr)^{2}+O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}).

(26)

Combined with
Proposition

5
, we have

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big).

Similarly, we have
\operatorname{E}(2\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})\geq 0
,

\operatorname{E}\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Bigl(\theta_{i}+\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}\Bigr)^{2}+O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}),

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big).

Step 2.
We prove (ii). By (
25
) and
\operatorname{E}(2\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle 2\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=

\displaystyle 2\operatorname{E}\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})+R_{N}^{(1)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big),

where
R_{N}^{(1)}=\operatorname{E}(2\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})\geq 0
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle 2\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=

\displaystyle 2E\tilde{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})+R_{N}^{(2)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big),

where
R_{N}^{(2)}=\operatorname{E}(2\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})-\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})\geq 0
.

Step 3.
We prove (iii). Combining (
25
) and (
26
), we have

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}\Bigr)^{2}+O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big).

If
\liminf N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}/\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\rightarrow\infty
, then,
O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=o(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})
. Therefore,

\hat{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}=\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}\Bigr)^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big).

The proof for

\tilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=\textnormal{Var}(\tilde{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\Bigl(\theta_{i}+\sum_{j}E_{ji}\theta_{\backslash\boldsymbol{W},j}\Bigr)^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}\Big)

is similar, so we omit it.

∎

Appendix F
Proof for the results in
Section

6

F.1
Proof of
Corollary

1

Proof.

Applying
Proposition

3
and
Proposition

4
with
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=M\binom{N_{M}}{2}/\binom{N}{2}=O(M^{-1})
, the conclusion follows.

∎

F.2
Proof of
Corollary

2

Note that if we use
(\boldsymbol{V}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{V}_{K})
in the regression, where
\boldsymbol{V}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{V}_{K}
are the top
K
eigenvectors, the corresponding
\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=O(\lambda_{K+1})
, where
\lambda_{K+1}
is the
(K+1)
-th largest eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}
.

Assume that we can further stratify the
M
groups into
K
strata:
\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{M}_{K}=\{1,\ldots,M\}
,
\mathcal{M}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{M}_{k_{2}}=\emptyset
for
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
. We assume that there is no between-group heterogeneity in each stratum; see
Assumption

15
below.

Assumption 15
.

If
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq M
, we have
N_{M}^{-1}\sum_{i:C_{i}=m}Y_{Z_{i}=z}=N_{M}^{-1}

\sum_{i:C_{i}=m^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
, for
z=0,1
. Moreover, there exist constants
0<\underline{c}\leq\bar{c}
, such that
\underline{c}M\leq|\mathcal{M}_{k}|\leq\bar{c}M
.

Under
Assumption

15
, we can merge the homogeneous groups into a stratum. Let
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}=(I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}),\ldots,I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{K}))
be the vector of merged group indicators. We use
\hat{e}_{i}
from the following regression in the eigenvector-adjusted IATE and GATE estimators:

\displaystyle Y_{i}\sim I(Z_{i}=0){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}+I(Z_{i}=1){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}.

(27)

Note that regression (
2
) in the main text under
Assumption

11
is a special case of (
27
) under
Assumption

15
. With a slight abuse of notation, we still denote the resulting eigenvector-adjusted IATE and GATE estimators as
\hat{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
and
\hat{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
, respectively.
Corollary

5
below is an extension of
Corollary

2
.

Corollary 5
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
,
6
–
10
, and
15
, we have
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(M^{-1/2})
and
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(M^{-1/2})
.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}\equiv(I(C_{i}=1),\ldots,I(C_{i}=M))
. We denote
\tilde{e}_{Z_{i}=z}
and
{e}_{Z_{i}=z}
as the projection residual of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}
on
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
and
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
, respectively.
Let
\mathcal{M}(i)
denote the stratum unit
i
belongs to, i.e.,
C_{i}\in\mathcal{M}(i)
. Then,

\displaystyle\tilde{e}_{Z_{i}=z}=Y_{Z_{i}=z}-N_{M}^{-1}\sum_{j:C_{j}=C_{i}}Y_{Z_{j}=z},

\displaystyle{e}_{Z_{i}=z}=Y_{Z_{i}=z}-(|\mathcal{M}(i)|N_{M})^{-1}\sum_{j:C_{j}\in\mathcal{M}(i)}Y_{Z_{j}=z}.

By
Assumption

15
, we have
\tilde{e}_{Z_{i}=z}={e}_{Z_{i}=z}
. We denote
\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
and
\tilde{\Delta}_{N}
as the quantities corresponding to
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
, respectively.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=\tilde{\Delta}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}.

Since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
is the top
M
eigenvectors of
\boldsymbol{E}=\textrm{diag}(\{\boldsymbol{J}_{N_{M}}-\boldsymbol{I}_{N_{M}}\}_{m=1}^{M})
and
\lambda_{M+1}(\boldsymbol{E})=-1
, it follows that
\tilde{\Delta}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=O(1)
. Using
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=M\binom{N_{M}}{2}/\binom{N}{2}=O(M^{-1})
,
N\geq M
, we have

O_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+\tilde{\Delta}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}/N)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(M^{-1}).

The conclusion follows.
∎

F.3
Proof of
Corollary

3

Proof.

Applying
Proposition

3
and
Proposition

4
with
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=\big\{N_{R}\binom{N_{C}}{2}+N_{R}\binom{N_{C}}{2}\big\}/N^{2}=O(N_{R}^{-1}+N_{C}^{-1})
, the conclusion follows.

∎

F.4
Proof of
Corollary

4

Assume that we can group the rows and columns into
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
groups, such that
Assumption

16
below holds:
\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{R}_{K_{1}}=\{1,\ldots,N_{R}\}
and
\mathcal{C}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{C}_{K_{2}}=\{1,\ldots,N_{C}\}
,
\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{R}_{k^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for
k\neq k^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}_{k}\cap\mathcal{C}_{k^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for
k\neq k^{\prime}
.

Assumption 16
.

If
r,r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{1}
, we have
N_{C}^{-1}\sum_{i:R_{i}=r}Y_{Z_{i}=z}=N_{C}^{-1}\sum_{i:R_{i}=r^{\prime}}

Y_{Z_{i}=z}
,
z=0,1
. If
c,c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{2}
, we have
N_{R}^{-1}\sum_{i:C_{i}=c}Y_{Z_{i}=z}=N_{R}^{-1}\sum_{i:C_{i}=c^{\prime}}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
,
z=0,1
. Moreover, there exist constants
0<\underline{c}\leq\bar{c}
, such that
\underline{c}N_{R}\leq|\mathcal{R}_{k}|\leq\bar{c}N_{R}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{1}
and
\underline{c}N_{C}\leq|\mathcal{C}_{k}|\leq\bar{c}N_{C}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{2}
.

Assumption

16
assumes seller homogeneity and buyer homogeneity within groups, which is a generalization of
Assumption

12
. With a slight abuse of notation, let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}\equiv(I(R_{i}=1),\ldots,I(R_{i}=N_{R}),I(C_{i}=1),\ldots,I(C_{i}=N_{C}))
and
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\equiv(I(R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}),\ldots,I(R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{K_{1}}),I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}),\ldots,I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{K_{2}}))
. We denote
\tilde{e}_{Z_{i}=z}
and
{e}_{Z_{i}=z}
as the residuals of the linear regression of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}
on
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
and
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
, respectively.

Under
Assumption

16
, we extend regression (
2
) into the following regression:

\displaystyle Y_{i}\sim I(Z_{i}=0){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}+I(Z_{i}=1){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}.

(28)

The above regression is an interacted two-way effect regression, which adjusts the effect of homogeneous buyer and seller groups under both treatment and control groups. Although
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
is rank deficient, the regression residual
\hat{e}_{i}
is well-defined as the residual of
Y_{i}
projected into the column space of
(I(Z_{i}=1){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i},I(Z_{i}=0){\boldsymbol{W}}_{i})
. When
K_{1}=K_{2}=1
, the residual obtained by
Equation

28
is equal to that of
Equation

2
in the main text. Hence,
Corollary

6
below is a generalization of
Corollary

4
. With a slight abuse of notation, we still denote the regression (
28
) adjusted IATE and GATE estimators as
\hat{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}
and
\hat{\tau}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}
, respectively.

Corollary 6
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
4
,
6
–
9
,
12
, and
16
, we have
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{IND}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N_{R}^{-1/2}+N_{C}^{-1/2})
and
\hat{\tau}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{{EV}}}-\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{TOT}}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N_{R}^{-1/2}+N_{C}^{-1/2})
.

Proof.

Denote
Y_{z,\mathcal{R}_{k}}=(N_{C}|\mathcal{R}_{k}|)^{-1}\sum_{i:R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{1}
and
Y_{z,\mathcal{C}_{k}}=(N_{C}|\mathcal{C}_{k}|)^{-1}

\sum_{i:R_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
for
1\leq k\leq K_{2}
. Denote
Y_{z,r}=N_{C}^{-1}\sum_{i:R_{i}=r}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
for
1\leq r\leq N_{R}
and
Y_{z,c}=N_{R}^{-1}\sum_{i:C_{i}=c}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
for
1\leq c\leq N_{C}
. Let
Y_{z}=N^{-1}\sum_{i}Y_{Z_{i}=z}
. Let
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}=(I(R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}),\ldots,I(R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{K_{1}}))^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{i}=(I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}),\ldots,I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{K_{2}}))^{\top}
. Let
\mathcal{R}(i)
and
\mathcal{C}(i)
denote the seller group and buyer group unit
i
belongs to, respectively, i.e.,
C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}(i)
and
R_{i}\in\mathcal{R}(i)
.

The projection residual of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}
on
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
is equal to
Y_{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{z,\mathcal{R}(i)}
. The projection residual of
\boldsymbol{V}_{i}
on
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
is equal to
\boldsymbol{V}_{i,\backslash\boldsymbol{U}}=(I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1})-|\mathcal{C}_{1}|/N_{C},\ldots,I(C_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{K_{2}})-|\mathcal{C}_{K_{2}}|/N_{C})^{\top}
.
Hence,
{e}_{Z_{i}=z}
, the residual of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}
on
(\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top},\boldsymbol{V}_{i}^{\top})
is equal to the residual of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{z,\mathcal{R}(i)}
on
\boldsymbol{V}_{i,\backslash\boldsymbol{U}}
, and equal to that of
Y_{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{z,\mathcal{R}(i)}
on
\boldsymbol{V}_{i}
, which is equal to
Y_{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{z,\mathcal{R}(i)}-Y_{z,\mathcal{C}(i)}+Y_{z}
. Similarly,

\tilde{e}_{i}=Y_{Z_{i}=z}-Y_{z,R_{i}}-Y_{z,C_{i}}+Y_{z}
. Under
Assumption

16
, we have
\tilde{e}_{Z_{i}=z}={e}_{Z_{i}=z}
.

We denote by
\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}
and
\tilde{\Delta}_{N}
the quantities corresponding to
\boldsymbol{W}_{i}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
, respectively. It is not difficult to show that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{E}=\sum_{r=1}^{N_{R}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{r,N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{C}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{e}_{r,N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{C}})+\sum_{c=1}^{N_{C}}(\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{c,N_{C}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{c,N_{C}})-2\boldsymbol{I}_{N}.

(29)

Therefore, the dominant eigenvectors of
\boldsymbol{E}
are in the space spanned by
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{r,N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{C}}\}_{r=1}^{N_{R}}\cup\{\boldsymbol{1}_{N_{R}}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{c,N_{C}}\}_{c=1}^{N_{c}}
, which is
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
. The eigenvalues of the eigenvectors orthogonal to
\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}
are
-2
. As a consequence, following the argument of Remark
7
,

\tilde{\Delta}_{N}=O(1),

which yields

\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=\tilde{\Delta}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=O(1).

On the other hand, we have
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=\big\{N_{R}\binom{N_{C}}{2}+N_{R}\binom{N_{C}}{2}\big\}/N^{2}=O(N_{R}^{-1}+N_{C}^{-1})
. Therefore,
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N_{R}^{-1}+N_{C}^{-1})
. The conclusion follows.
∎

F.5
Proof of
Proposition

6

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\lambda}_{k}
,
k=1,\ldots,N
, be the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{E}
in a decreasing order. Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{k}=(\psi_{k}(U_{i}))_{i=1}^{n}
,
k=1,\ldots,r
, and define

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{G}:=(G(U_{i},U_{j}))_{1\leq i,j\leq N}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{k}^{\top}.

(30)

Note that
\boldsymbol{G}_{ii}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\psi_{k}(U_{i})
and is therefore not necessarily
0
. Let
\lambda^{\star}_{1}\geq\ldots\geq\lambda^{\star}_{K}\geq 0\geq\lambda^{\star}_{K+1}\geq\ldots\geq\lambda^{\star}_{N}
be the eigenvalues of
(\rho^{\star}_{N})\boldsymbol{G}
, where
K
is the number of positive eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{G}
. Since, by
Equation

30
,
\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{G})=r
, there are at most
r
eigenvalues in
\lambda^{\star}_{i}
that are not equal to
0
. By Weyl’s inequality, we have, for
i=1,\ldots,N

|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-{\lambda}_{i}^{\star}|\leq\|(\rho^{\star}_{N})\boldsymbol{G}-\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{2}\sqrt{N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}}),

where the last equality follows from Lemma 25 of
Li & Wager (2022)
. Let
|\tilde{\lambda}_{(r+1)}|
be the
(r+1)
-th largest eigenvalue of
|\tilde{\lambda}_{k}|
,
k=1,\ldots,N
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{\lambda}_{(r+1)}|=

\displaystyle\max_{\mathcal{K}\in[N]:|\mathcal{K}|=r+1}\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|\tilde{\lambda}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\mathcal{K}\in[N]:|\mathcal{K}|=r+1}\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|{\lambda}_{k}^{\star}|+\|(\rho^{\star}_{N})\boldsymbol{G}-\boldsymbol{E}\|_{\operatorname{op}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+O_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{2}\sqrt{N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{2}\sqrt{N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}}).

Note that
|\tilde{\lambda}_{(r+1)}|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{4}N\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})
is the
(r+1)
-th largest eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{E}^{2}
, thus,
N^{-1}\Delta_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=O(N^{-1}\tilde{\lambda}_{(r+1)}^{2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{4}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})=o_{\mathbb{P}}((\log N)^{4+\delta}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}),
for any
\delta>0
.

By definition,
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=\sum_{i,j}E_{ij}/N^{2}
. Then,
N^{2}\operatorname{E}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}=N(N-1)\operatorname{E}(E_{ij})
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\textnormal{Var}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}})=N^{-4}\sum_{i,j}\textnormal{Cov}(E_{ij})+N^{-4}\sum_{i,j,k}\textnormal{Cov}(E_{ij},E_{ik}).

By Lemma 16 of
Li & Wager (2022)
, we have
\operatorname{E}(E_{ij})/\{\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}\operatorname{E}G(U_{1},U_{2})\}\rightarrow 1
and
\operatorname{E}E_{ij}E_{ik}/

\{(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})^{2}\operatorname{E}G(U_{1},U_{2})G(U_{2},U_{3})\}\rightarrow 1
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\textnormal{Cov}(E_{ij})=\operatorname{E}E_{ij}-(\operatorname{E}E_{ij})^{2}=O(\operatorname{E}E_{ij})=O(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}),

\displaystyle\textnormal{Cov}(E_{ij},E_{ik})=\operatorname{E}E_{ij}E_{ik}-(\operatorname{E}E_{ij})^{2}=O((\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})^{2}),

which yield that
\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}/\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}=\{\operatorname{E}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1}(\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})^{1/2}+N^{-1/2}\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star})\}/\rho_{\lx@scalerel@obj{N}}^{\star}=\operatorname{E}G(U_{1},U_{2})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),
where the last equality follows from
Assumption

14
(iv).
∎
